### S1-01 | A/R Summary opens with customers in name order, A to Z
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have four customers with open invoices on today's date, named "10 Trucking" (customer A), "2 Trucking" (customer B), "A-1 Towing" (customer C) and "a1 towing" (customer D). Their names differ by a number, a dash or capitals.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Summary (Reports, then "A/R Aging Summary" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Look at the header of the customer name column (the first column).
  2. Without clicking any column header, find the four customers in the list (scroll down until they load if they are further down).
  Step 1:
    - The customer name column (the first column) is marked as the sorted column (a small arrow beside its heading).
  Step 2:
    - All four customers are listed, reading from top to bottom customer A, then customer B, then customer C, then customer D (other customers may sit between them).
    - "10 Trucking" comes before "2 Trucking" because names compare character by character.
    - "A-1 Towing" comes before "a1 towing" because capitals are ignored and the dash compares as a character.
### S1-02 | A/P Summary opens with vendors in name order, A to Z
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have four vendors with open bills on today's date, named "10 Trucking" (vendor A), "2 Trucking" (vendor B), "A-1 Towing" (vendor C) and "a1 towing" (vendor D). Their names differ by a number, a dash or capitals.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/P Summary (Reports, then "A/P Aging Summary" under ACCOUNTS PAYABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Look at the header of the vendor name column (the first column).
  2. Without clicking any column header, find the four vendors in the list (scroll down until they load if they are further down).
  Step 1:
    - The vendor name column (the first column) is marked as the sorted column (a small arrow beside its heading).
  Step 2:
    - All four vendors are listed, reading from top to bottom vendor A, then vendor B, then vendor C, then vendor D (other vendors may sit between them).
    - "10 Trucking" comes before "2 Trucking" because names compare character by character.
    - "A-1 Towing" comes before "a1 towing" because capitals are ignored and the dash compares as a character.
### S1-03 | A/R Summary sorts accented customer names with their base letter
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have six customers with open invoices on today's date, named "Eagle" (customer A), "Émile's" (customer B), "Emma" (customer C), "Fargo" (customer D), "Øster" (customer E) and "Ozone" (customer F). Customer B and customer E start with a capital accented letter.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Summary (Reports, then "A/R Aging Summary" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Without clicking any column header, find the six customers in the list.
  Step 1:
    - All six customers are listed, reading from top to bottom customer A, customer B, customer C, customer D, customer E, customer F.
    - "Émile's" sorts among the E names (between "Eagle" and "Emma"), not after the Z names.
    - "Øster" sorts as an O, before "Ozone".
### S1-04 | A/P Summary sorts lower-case accented and joined letters as plain letters
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have six vendors with open bills on today's date, named "Ackley" (vendor A), "æble" (vendor B), "Afton" (vendor C), "Strand" (vendor D), "Straße" (vendor E) and "Strasser" (vendor F). Vendor B has "æ" and vendor E has "ß" in its name.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/P Summary (Reports, then "A/P Aging Summary" under ACCOUNTS PAYABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Without clicking any column header, find the six vendors in the list.
  Step 1:
    - All six vendors are listed, reading from top to bottom vendor A, vendor B, vendor C, vendor D, vendor E, vendor F.
    - "æble" sorts as "aeble" (between "Ackley" and "Afton").
    - "Straße" sorts as "Strasse" (after "Strand", before "Strasser").
### S1-05 | Two customer names that differ only in capitals keep one order everywhere
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have two customers with open invoices on today's date, whose names differ only in capitals: customer A and customer B.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Summary (Reports, then "A/R Aging Summary" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Find customer A and customer B in the list and note which one is higher.
  2. Choose "Download (PDF)" from the ⋯ menu at the right of the title.
  3. Open the PDF and find the two names.
  4. Choose "Download (CSV)" from the ⋯ menu.
  5. Open the CSV in a spreadsheet program and find the two names.
  6. Reload the page and find the two names again.
  Step 1:
    - The two customers sit next to each other (no other customer between them) in one order.
  Step 2:
    - The PDF downloads.
  Step 3:
    - The two names are in the same order as on screen.
  Step 4:
    - The CSV downloads.
  Step 5:
    - The two rows are in the same order as on screen.
  Step 6:
    - After the reload they are still in the same order.

### S1-07 | A/R Summary lists Emile's Towing before Émile's Towing
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have two customers with open invoices on today's date whose names differ only by an accent: customer A, named "Emile's Towing", without it, and customer B, named "Émile's Towing", with it.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Summary (Reports, then "A/R Aging Summary" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Without clicking any column header, find customer A and customer B in the list.
  Step 1:
    - The two customers sit next to each other (with the accent folded to its base letter their names are the same).
    - Customer A (no accent) is directly above customer B (with the accent).
    - The tie is broken by the plain lower-cased names, where the unaccented letter comes first.
### S1-08 | A/R Detail lists the five buckets in fixed order and rows by name
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Make sure the report has open documents in all five aging buckets on today's date.
  P: Make sure that in the "1-30 days past due" bucket, customer A has one open invoice, invoice A, and customer B has two, invoice B and invoice C. Customer B's name comes first alphabetically, and invoice B's number is smaller than invoice C's but has fewer digits.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Detail (Reports, then "A/R Aging Detail" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Read the bucket rows from top to bottom without expanding anything.
  2. Look at the header of the "Customer Full Name" column.
  3. Click the chevron of the "1-30 days past due" bucket to expand it.
  Step 1:
    - The buckets read, in this order: "91 or more days past due", "61-90 days past due", "31-60 days past due", "1-30 days past due", "Current".
  Step 2:
    - "Customer Full Name" is marked as the sorted column (a small arrow beside its heading).
  Step 3:
    - The bucket opens and shows its rows: customer B's two invoices come first, invoice B above invoice C (smaller number first, counted as a number), then customer A's invoice A.
### S1-09 | A/P Detail lists the five buckets in fixed order and rows by name
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Make sure the report has open documents in all five aging buckets on today's date.
  P: Make sure that in the "1-30 days past due" bucket, vendor A has one open bill, bill A, and vendor B has two, bill B and bill C. Vendor B's name comes first alphabetically, and bill B's number is smaller than bill C's but has fewer digits.

  P: Open A/P Detail (Reports, then "A/P Aging Detail" under ACCOUNTS PAYABLE in the left menu).

  1. Read the bucket rows from top to bottom without expanding anything.
  2. Look at the header of the "Vendor Name" column.
  3. Click the chevron of the "1-30 days past due" bucket to expand it.
  Step 1:
    - The buckets read, in this order: "91 or more days past due", "61-90 days past due", "31-60 days past due", "1-30 days past due", "Current".
  Step 2:
    - "Vendor Name" is marked as the sorted column (a small arrow beside its heading).

### S1-10 | A/R Collection groups customers by name, rows oldest date first
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have two customers with overdue invoices on today's date: customer A and customer B; customer B's name comes first alphabetically.
  P: Make sure customer B has three overdue invoices: invoice A with the oldest date, and invoice B and invoice C with the same later date. Invoice B's number is smaller than invoice C's but has fewer digits, and invoice C was invoiced earlier that day than invoice B.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Collection (Reports, then "A/R Aging Collection" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Find the groups of customer A and customer B and note their order.
  2. Look at the header of the "Date" column.
  3. Expand customer B's group.
  Step 1:
    - Customer B's group is above customer A's group.
  Step 2:
    - "Date" is marked as the sorted column (a small arrow beside its heading).
  Step 3:
    - The group opens: invoice A first (oldest date), then invoice B, then invoice C: on the same date the smaller number comes first, counted as a number, whatever the time of day each was invoiced.
### S1-11 | A/P Unpaid Invoices groups vendors by name, rows oldest date first
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have two vendors with overdue bills on today's date: vendor A and vendor B; vendor B's name comes first alphabetically.
  P: Make sure vendor B has three overdue bills: bill A with the oldest date, and bill B and bill C with the same later date. Bill B's number is smaller than bill C's but has fewer digits, and bill C was entered earlier that day than bill B.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/P Unpaid Invoices (Reports, then "A/P Unpaid Invoices" under ACCOUNTS PAYABLE in the left menu; the page title reads "A/P Unpaid Invoices Report").
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Find the groups of vendor A and vendor B and note their order.
  2. Look at the header of the "Date" column.
  3. Expand vendor B's group.
  Step 1:
    - Vendor B's group is above vendor A's group.
  Step 2:
    - "Date" is marked as the sorted column (a small arrow beside its heading).
  Step 3:
    - The group opens: bill A first (oldest date), then bill B, then bill C: on the same date the smaller number comes first, counted as a number, whatever the time of day each was entered.
### S1-12 | Each A/R report opens with its opening column marked as sorted
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Make sure each of the three A/R reports has open documents on today's date.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open Reports (top menu; its left menu lists the A/R reports under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE and the A/P reports under ACCOUNTS PAYABLE).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Open A/R Summary and look at the column headers.
  2. Open A/R Detail and look at the column headers.
  3. Open A/R Collection and look at the column headers.
  Step 1:
    - The customer name column (the first column) is marked as the sorted column (a small arrow beside its heading).
    - No other column is marked as sorted.
  Step 2:
    - "Customer Full Name" is marked as the sorted column (a small arrow beside its heading).
    - No other column is marked as sorted.
  Step 3:
    - "Date" is marked as the sorted column (a small arrow beside its heading).
    - No other column is marked as sorted.

### S1-14 | Without Reports page access the six reports are not available
  P: Sign in as user B, whose custom role has every permission except "Reports → Page Access".
  P: Make sure you have a link to A/R Summary and a link to A/P Detail, copied earlier from the address bar by an Admin user.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open the ShopView home page.
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Click Reports in the top menu and read the left menu's ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE and ACCOUNTS PAYABLE sections.
  2. Paste the A/R Summary link into the address bar.
  3. Press Enter.
  4. Paste the A/P Detail link into the address bar.
  5. Press Enter.
  Step 1:
    - None of the six reports is offered (on screen: "A/R Aging Summary", "A/R Aging Detail", "A/R Aging Collection", "A/P Aging Summary", "A/P Aging Detail", "A/P Unpaid Invoices"), as today.
  Step 2:
    - The A/R Summary link shows in the address bar.
  Step 3:
    - The report does not open, as today for a user without this permission.
  Step 4:
    - The A/P Detail link shows in the address bar.
  Step 5:
    - The report does not open, as today.
### S1-15 | A customer with nothing open on the as-of date is not listed
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have a customer, customer A, with no open documents: its only invoice is paid in full.
  P: Have a customer, customer B, with one open invoice, created today, and nothing before today.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Summary (Reports, then "A/R Aging Summary" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Look for customer A and customer B in the list.
  2. Change the as-of date to yesterday.
  Step 1:
    - Customer A is not listed, as today.
    - Customer B is listed.
  Step 2:
    - The report reloads for yesterday.
    - Customer B is not listed: on yesterday's date it had nothing open.
### S1-16 | A/R Summary sorts names that start with a punctuation mark by character
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have four customers with open invoices on today's date: customer A, customer B, customer C and customer D. After the same first words, customer A's name goes on with "#", customer B's with "-", customer C's with a digit and customer D's with "@".
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Summary (Reports, then "A/R Aging Summary" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Without clicking any column header, find the four customers in the list.
  Step 1:
    - All four customers are listed, reading from top to bottom customer A, customer B, customer C, customer D: plain character order, where "#" comes before "-", both come before the digits.
    - "@" comes after them.
### S2-01 | A/R Summary: the customer name header reverses on its first click
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have three customers with open invoices on today's date: customer A, customer B and customer C; in name order they read customer A, customer B, customer C.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Summary (Reports, then "A/R Aging Summary" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Click the header of the customer name column (the first column).
  2. Click the header of the customer name column (the first column) again.
  Step 1:
    - The list was already sorted by name, so this first click reverses it: Z to A, customer C above customer B above customer A.
  Step 2:
    - Back to A to Z: customer A, customer B, customer C.
### S2-01b | A/R Summary: the Location header sorts A to Z, then Z to A
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have open documents in these reports at two locations, location A and location B, with location A's name first in A to Z order.
  P: Make sure you are enrolled at both locations.
  P: Have three customers with open invoices on today's date: customer A only at location B, customer B at both locations and customer C only at location A. Both location names come after the word "Multiple" in A to Z order.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Summary (Reports, then "A/R Aging Summary" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Click the "Location" header.
  2. Click the "Location" header again.
  3. Click the "Location" header a third time.
  Step 1:
    - The list sorts by Location, A to Z, comparing the words as they read: customer B ("Multiple"), then customer C (location A), then customer A (location B).
  Step 2:
    - Z to A: customer A, customer C, customer B.
  Step 3:
    - The same as step 1, A to Z again.
    - There is no unsorted state.
### S2-02 | A/R Summary: Current, 1-30 Days and 31-60 Days sort largest first
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have three customers, customer A, customer B and customer C, with open invoices on today's date with these open amounts per column:
  P: Customer A: Current $100.00, 1-30 Days $200.00, 31-60 Days $300.00, 61-90 Days $250.00, 91 And Over $350.00, Total $1,200.00.
  P: Customer B: Current $300.00, 1-30 Days $100.00, 31-60 Days $200.00, 61-90 Days $350.00, 91 And Over $150.00, Total $1,100.00.
  P: Customer C: Current $200.00, 1-30 Days $300.00, 31-60 Days $100.00, 61-90 Days $150.00, 91 And Over $250.00, Total $1,000.00.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Summary (Reports, then "A/R Aging Summary" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Click the "Current" header.
  2. Click the "Current" header again.
  3. Click the "Current" header a third time.
  4. Click the "1-30 Days" header.
  5. Click the "1-30 Days" header again.
  6. Click the "1-30 Days" header a third time.
  7. Click the "31-60 Days" header.
  8. Click the "31-60 Days" header again.
  9. Click the "31-60 Days" header a third time.
  Step 1:
    - The list sorts by Current, largest first: customer B $300.00, customer C $200.00, customer A $100.00.
  Step 2:
    - Reversed, smallest first: customer A, customer C, customer B.
  Step 3:
    - Largest first again, as in step 1.
  Step 4:
    - The list sorts by 1-30 Days, largest first: customer C $300.00, customer A $200.00, customer B $100.00.
  Step 5:
    - Reversed, smallest first: customer B, customer A, customer C.
  Step 6:
    - Largest first again, as in step 4.
  Step 7:
    - The list sorts by 31-60 Days, largest first: customer A $300.00, customer B $200.00, customer C $100.00.
  Step 8:
    - Reversed, smallest first: customer C, customer B, customer A.
  Step 9:
    - Largest first again, as in step 7.
### S2-03 | A/R Summary: 61-90 Days, 91 And Over and Total sort largest first
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have three customers, customer A, customer B and customer C, with open invoices on today's date with these open amounts per column:
  P: Customer A: Current $100.00, 1-30 Days $200.00, 31-60 Days $300.00, 61-90 Days $250.00, 91 And Over $350.00, Total $1,200.00.
  P: Customer B: Current $300.00, 1-30 Days $100.00, 31-60 Days $200.00, 61-90 Days $350.00, 91 And Over $150.00, Total $1,100.00.
  P: Customer C: Current $200.00, 1-30 Days $300.00, 31-60 Days $100.00, 61-90 Days $150.00, 91 And Over $250.00, Total $1,000.00.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Summary (Reports, then "A/R Aging Summary" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Click the "61-90 Days" header.
  2. Click the "61-90 Days" header again.
  3. Click the "61-90 Days" header a third time.
  4. Click the "91 And Over" header.
  5. Click the "91 And Over" header again.
  6. Click the "91 And Over" header a third time.
  7. Click the "Total" header.
  8. Click the "Total" header again.
  9. Click the "Total" header a third time.
  Step 1:
    - The list sorts by 61-90 Days, largest first: customer B $350.00, customer A $250.00, customer C $150.00.
  Step 2:
    - Reversed, smallest first: customer C, customer A, customer B.
  Step 3:
    - Largest first again, as in step 1.
  Step 4:
    - The list sorts by 91 And Over, largest first: customer A $350.00, customer C $250.00, customer B $150.00.
  Step 5:
    - Reversed, smallest first: customer B, customer C, customer A.
  Step 6:
    - Largest first again, as in step 4.
  Step 7:
    - The list sorts by Total, largest first: customer A $1,200.00, customer B $1,100.00, customer C $1,000.00.
  Step 8:
    - Reversed, smallest first: customer C, customer B, customer A.
  Step 9:
    - Largest first again, as in step 7.

### S2-04b | A/P Summary: the Location header sorts A to Z, then Z to A
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have open documents in these reports at two locations, location A and location B, with location A's name first in A to Z order.
  P: Make sure you are enrolled at both locations.
  P: Have three vendors with open bills on today's date: vendor A only at location B, vendor B at both locations and vendor C only at location A. Both location names come after the word "Multiple" in A to Z order.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/P Summary (Reports, then "A/P Aging Summary" under ACCOUNTS PAYABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Click the "Location" header.
  2. Click the "Location" header again.
  3. Click the "Location" header a third time.
  Step 1:
    - The list sorts by Location, A to Z, comparing the words as they read: vendor B ("Multiple"), then vendor C (location A), then vendor A (location B).
  Step 2:
    - Z to A: vendor A, vendor C, vendor B.
  Step 3:
    - The same as step 1, A to Z again.
    - There is no unsorted state.
### S2-05 | A/P Summary: Current, 1-30 Days and 31-60 Days sort largest first
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have three vendors, vendor A, vendor B and vendor C, with open bills on today's date with these open amounts per column:
  P: Vendor A: Current $100.00, 1-30 Days $200.00, 31-60 Days $300.00, 61-90 Days $250.00, 91 And Over $350.00, Total $1,200.00.
  P: Vendor B: Current $300.00, 1-30 Days $100.00, 31-60 Days $200.00, 61-90 Days $350.00, 91 And Over $150.00, Total $1,100.00.
  P: Vendor C: Current $200.00, 1-30 Days $300.00, 31-60 Days $100.00, 61-90 Days $150.00, 91 And Over $250.00, Total $1,000.00.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/P Summary (Reports, then "A/P Aging Summary" under ACCOUNTS PAYABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Click the "Current" header.
  2. Click the "Current" header again.
  3. Click the "Current" header a third time.
  4. Click the "1-30 Days" header.
  5. Click the "1-30 Days" header again.
  6. Click the "1-30 Days" header a third time.
  7. Click the "31-60 Days" header.
  8. Click the "31-60 Days" header again.
  9. Click the "31-60 Days" header a third time.
  Step 1:
    - The list sorts by Current, largest first: vendor B $300.00, vendor C $200.00, vendor A $100.00.
  Step 2:
    - Reversed, smallest first: vendor A, vendor C, vendor B.
  Step 3:
    - Largest first again, as in step 1.
  Step 4:
    - The list sorts by 1-30 Days, largest first: vendor C $300.00, vendor A $200.00, vendor B $100.00.
  Step 5:
    - Reversed, smallest first: vendor B, vendor A, vendor C.
  Step 6:
    - Largest first again, as in step 4.
  Step 7:
    - The list sorts by 31-60 Days, largest first: vendor A $300.00, vendor B $200.00, vendor C $100.00.
  Step 8:
    - Reversed, smallest first: vendor C, vendor B, vendor A.
  Step 9:
    - Largest first again, as in step 7.

### S2-07 | A/R Detail: the text column headers sort A to Z, then Z to A
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have open documents in these reports at two locations, location A and location B, with location A's name first in A to Z order.
  P: Make sure you are enrolled at both locations.
  P: Make sure that in the "1-30 days past due" bucket there are three open documents: document A for customer C at location B, and document B for customer A and document C for customer B at location A; one of the three is a different transaction type from the others.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Detail (Reports, then "A/R Aging Detail" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: Leave the report with the "1-30 days past due" bucket expanded.
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Click the "Location" header.
  2. Click the "Location" header again.
  3. Click the "Location" header a third time.
  4. Click the "Transaction Type" header.
  5. Click the "Transaction Type" header again.
  Step 1:
    - Rows inside each aging bucket sort by Location, A to Z: in the "1-30 days past due" bucket the location A rows come before the location B row.
  Step 2:
    - Z to A: the location B row comes first.
  Step 3:
    - A to Z again, as in step 1.
    - There is no unsorted state.
  Step 4:
    - Rows inside each aging bucket sort by Transaction Type, A to Z: read the Transaction Type words top to bottom in the "1-30 days past due" bucket.
    - They are in alphabetical order.
  Step 5:
    - Z to A: the same words in reverse alphabetical order.
### S2-07b | A/R Detail: the Customer Full Name header reverses on its first click
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Make sure that in the "1-30 days past due" bucket there are three open documents: document A for customer C, document B for customer A and document C for customer B; in name order the customers read customer A, customer B, customer C.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Detail (Reports, then "A/R Aging Detail" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: Leave the report with the "1-30 days past due" bucket expanded.
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Click the "Customer Full Name" header.
  2. Click the "Customer Full Name" header again.
  Step 1:
    - The report was already sorted by Customer Full Name, so this first click reverses it: in the "1-30 days past due" bucket, document A (customer C) first, then document C (customer B), then document B (customer A).
  Step 2:
    - A to Z again: document B, document C, document A.
### S2-08 | A/R Detail: Date and Due Date sort oldest first, then newest first
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Make sure that in the "1-30 days past due" bucket there are three open invoices, document A, document B and document C for customer C, customer A and customer B in that order (in name order these customers read A, B, C):
  P: Document A is dated 40 days ago, due 10 days ago, amount $300.00, open $50.00.
  P: Document B is dated 20 days ago, due 20 days ago, amount $100.00, open $100.00.
  P: Document C is dated 35 days ago, due 5 days ago, amount $200.00, open $150.00.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Detail (Reports, then "A/R Aging Detail" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: Leave the report with the "1-30 days past due" bucket expanded.
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Click the "Date" header.
  2. Click the "Date" header again.
  3. Click the "Date" header a third time.
  4. Click the "Due Date" header.
  5. Click the "Due Date" header again.
  Step 1:
    - Rows inside each aging bucket sort by Date, oldest first: in the "1-30 days past due" bucket, document A, document C, document B.
  Step 2:
    - Newest first: document B, document C, document A.
  Step 3:
    - Oldest first again, as in step 1.
  Step 4:
    - Rows inside each aging bucket sort by Due Date, oldest first: document B, document A, document C.
  Step 5:
    - Newest first: document C, document A, document B.

### S2-10 | A/R Detail: ID sorts by number value, and ties go by number
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Make sure that in the "1-30 days past due" bucket there are three open invoices with the same due date, document A, document B and document C, whose numbers have different digit counts, so that in number order they read document A, document B, document C but in letter-by-letter order document B, document C, document A.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Detail (Reports, then "A/R Aging Detail" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: Leave the report with the "1-30 days past due" bucket expanded.
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Click the "ID" header.
  2. Click the "ID" header again.
  3. Click the "Due Date" header.
  4. Click the "Due Date" header again.
  Step 1:
    - Rows inside each aging bucket sort by ID as numbers, never letter by letter, smallest number first: document A, document B, document C.
  Step 2:
    - Largest number first: document C, document B, document A, still in number order.
  Step 3:
    - Rows inside each aging bucket sort by Due Date, oldest first.
    - Document A, document B and document C tie on Due Date, so they are ordered by number, smallest first: document A, document B, document C.
  Step 4:
    - The Due Date sort reverses, newest first.
    - The three still tie and keep the same order, smallest number first: document A, document B, document C (reversing a sort reverses the sorted column only).

    - Rows inside each aging bucket sort by Past Due, largest first: document B (20 days), document A (10 days), document C (5 days).
  Step 2:
    - Smallest first: document C, document A, document B.
  Step 3:
    - Rows inside each aging bucket sort by Amount, largest first: document A $300.00, document C $200.00, document B $100.00.
  Step 4:
    - Smallest first: document B, document C, document A.
  Step 5:
    - Rows inside each aging bucket sort by Open Balance, largest first: document C $150.00, document B $100.00, document A $50.00.
  Step 6:
    - Smallest first: document A, document B, document C.
  Step 7:
    - Largest first again, as in step 5.
### S2-18 | A/P Detail: No. sorts by number value, and ties go by number
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Make sure that in the "1-30 days past due" bucket there are three open bills with the same due date, document A, document B and document C, whose numbers have different digit counts, so that in number order they read document A, document B, document C but in letter-by-letter order document B, document C, document A.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/P Detail (Reports, then "A/P Aging Detail" under ACCOUNTS PAYABLE in the left menu).
  P: Leave the report with the "1-30 days past due" bucket expanded.
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Click the "No." header.
  2. Click the "No." header again.
  3. Click the "Due Date" header.
  4. Click the "Due Date" header again.
  Step 1:
    - Rows inside each aging bucket sort by No. as numbers, never letter by letter, smallest number first: document A, document B, document C.
  Step 2:
    - Largest number first: document C, document B, document A, still in number order.
  Step 3:
    - Rows inside each aging bucket sort by Due Date, oldest first.
    - Document A, document B and document C tie on Due Date, so they are ordered by number, smallest first: document A, document B, document C.
  Step 4:
    - The Due Date sort reverses, newest first.
    - The three still tie and keep the same order, smallest number first: document A, document B, document C (reversing a sort reverses the sorted column only).
### S2-19 | A/P Unpaid Invoices: the text column headers sort A to Z, then Z to A
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have open documents in these reports at two locations, location A and location B, with location A's name first in A to Z order.
  P: Make sure you are enrolled at both locations.
  P: Have a vendor, vendor A, with overdue bills on today's date.
  P: Make sure that in vendor A's group there are three open documents: document A at location B, and document B and document C at location A; one of the three is a different transaction type from the others.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/P Unpaid Invoices (Reports, then "A/P Unpaid Invoices" under ACCOUNTS PAYABLE in the left menu; the page title reads "A/P Unpaid Invoices Report").
  P: Leave the report with vendor A's group expanded.
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Click the "Location" header.
  2. Click the "Location" header again.
  3. Click the "Location" header a third time.
  4. Click the "Transaction Type" header.
  5. Click the "Transaction Type" header again.
  Step 1:
    - Rows inside each vendor group sort by Location, A to Z: in vendor A's group the location A rows come before the location B row.
  Step 2:
    - Z to A: the location B row comes first.
  Step 3:
    - A to Z again, as in step 1.
    - There is no unsorted state.
  Step 4:
    - Rows inside each vendor group sort by Transaction Type, A to Z: read the Transaction Type words top to bottom in vendor A's group.
    - They are in alphabetical order.
  Step 5:
    - Z to A: the same words in reverse alphabetical order.

  Step 2:
    - Oldest first again: document A, document C, document B.

### S2-23 | A/P Unpaid Invoices: a vendor credit stays at the bottom when Due Date is sorted
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.

  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/P Unpaid Invoices (Reports, then "A/P Unpaid Invoices" under ACCOUNTS PAYABLE in the left menu; the page title reads "A/P Unpaid Invoices Report").

  2. Click the "Due Date" header.
  3. Click the "Due Date" header again.
  4. Click the "Past Due" header.
  5. Click the "Past Due" header again.
  Step 1:
    - The group shows the two bills and the vendor credit.
    - Vendor credit A's Due Date and Past Due cells hold no date or number (the screen draws a dash, "—"): a vendor credit has no due date and no Past Due here.
  Step 2:
    - Bill B, then bill A, then vendor credit A at the bottom of the group.
  Step 3:
    - Bill A, then bill B.
    - Vendor credit A still at the bottom.
  Step 4:
    - Bill B (20 days), then bill A (10 days), then vendor credit A at the bottom.
  Step 5:
    - Bill A, then bill B.
    - Vendor credit A still at the bottom.

    - Vendor credit A still sorts by its date.
    - It is not held at the bottom.
### S2-25 | A/R Detail: sorting moves rows, never groups, and keeps groups open

  2. Click the "Open Balance" header again.
  3. Click the "Date" header.

    - Rows re-sort inside each aging bucket, largest first.
    - The five buckets keep their fixed order (91 or more, 61-90, 31-60, 1-30, Current).
  Step 2:
    - Rows re-sort smallest first.
    - The five buckets keep their fixed order (91 or more, 61-90, 31-60, 1-30, Current).
  Step 3:
    - Rows re-sort by Date.
    - The five buckets keep their fixed order (91 or more, 61-90, 31-60, 1-30, Current).
    - "31-60 days past due" and "1-30 days past due" are still expanded and the others still collapsed: re-sorting never expands or collapses a group.

### S2-29 | A/R Summary: the sort stays while changing date, scrolling and filters
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have open documents in these reports at two locations, location A and location B, with location A's name first in A to Z order.
  P: Make sure you are enrolled at both locations.
  P: Make sure the report has more than 30 customers with open documents on today's date and on yesterday's date, at both locations.
  P: Have a customer, customer A, with open documents at both locations.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Summary (Reports, then "A/R Aging Summary" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Click the "Total" header once.
  2. Change the as-of date to yesterday.
  3. Scroll down until the next 30 customers load.
  4. Open the Customer filter.
  5. Tick customer A.
  6. In the Location filter, untick location B.
  Step 1:
    - The rows sort by Total, largest first.
    - The header shows it.
  Step 2:
    - The report reloads for yesterday and is still sorted by Total, largest first.
  Step 3:
    - The new rows are still sorted by Total, largest first.
  Step 4:
    - The Customer menu opens.
  Step 5:
    - Only customer A's rows remain, still sorted by Total, largest first.
  Step 6:
    - Only location A rows remain, still sorted by Total, largest first.
    - "Total" is still marked as the sorted column (a small arrow beside its heading).

    - The header shows it.
  Step 2:
    - The report reloads for yesterday and is still sorted by Open Balance, largest first.
  Step 3:
    - The bucket opens and shows its rows.
  Step 4:
    - The new rows are still sorted by Open Balance, largest first.
  Step 5:
    - The Vendor menu opens.
  Step 6:
    - Only vendor A's rows remain (expand a group to see them: a filter change collapses every group), still sorted by Open Balance, largest first.
  Step 7:
    - Only location A rows remain (expand a group to see them: a filter change collapses every group), still sorted by Open Balance, largest first.
    - "Open Balance" is still marked as the sorted column (a small arrow beside its heading).
### S2-34 | A/P Unpaid Invoices: the sort stays while changing date, scrolling and filters
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have open documents in these reports at two locations, location A and location B, with location A's name first in A to Z order.
  P: Make sure you are enrolled at both locations.
  P: Make sure the report has more than 30 vendors with open documents on today's date and on yesterday's date, at both locations.
  P: Have a vendor, vendor A, with open documents at both locations.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/P Unpaid Invoices (Reports, then "A/P Unpaid Invoices" under ACCOUNTS PAYABLE in the left menu; the page title reads "A/P Unpaid Invoices Report").
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Click the "Open Balance" header once.
  2. Change the as-of date to yesterday.
  3. Scroll down until the next 30 vendors load.
  4. Open the Vendor filter.
  5. Tick vendor A.
  6. In the Location filter, untick location B.
  Step 1:
    - The rows sort by Open Balance, largest first.
    - The header shows it.
  Step 2:
    - The report reloads for yesterday and is still sorted by Open Balance, largest first.
  Step 3:
    - The new rows are still sorted by Open Balance, largest first.
  Step 4:
    - The Vendor menu opens.
  Step 5:
    - Only vendor A's rows remain (expand a group to see them: a filter change collapses every group), still sorted by Open Balance, largest first.
  Step 6:
    - Only location A rows remain (expand a group to see them: a filter change collapses every group), still sorted by Open Balance, largest first.
    - "Open Balance" is still marked as the sorted column (a small arrow beside its heading).
### S2-35 | A/R Collection: changing the Past due filter keeps the sort
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have a customer, customer A, with overdue invoices on today's date: one 1 to 30 days past due, and two 31 to 60 days past due with different open balances.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Collection (Reports, then "A/R Aging Collection" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: Leave the report with customer A's group expanded.
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Click the "Open Balance" header once.
  2. In the Past due filter, tick "31-60 days".
  3. Expand customer A's group.
  Step 1:
    - The rows in customer A's group sort by Open Balance, largest first.
    - "Open Balance" is marked as the sorted column (a small arrow beside its heading).
  Step 2:
    - The report reloads with only the invoices 31 to 60 days past due (and any credits) left.
    - Every group is collapsed.
  Step 3:
    - The rows in the group are still sorted by Open Balance, largest first.
    - "Open Balance" is still marked as the sorted column (a small arrow beside its heading).

### S2-40 | A/R Detail: column headings are in Title Case on screen, PDF and CSV
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have open documents in these reports at two locations, location A and location B, with location A's name first in A to Z order.
  P: Make sure you are enrolled at both locations.
  P: Make sure the report has open documents at both locations on today's date.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Detail (Reports, then "A/R Aging Detail" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: Leave the report with every group expanded (Expand all).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Read the column headings, letter for letter, capitals included.
  2. Choose "Download (PDF)" from the ⋯ menu.
  3. Open the PDF and read its column headings.
  4. Choose "Download (CSV)" from the ⋯ menu.
  5. Open the CSV in a plain text editor and read its column heading row.
  Step 1:
    - "Date", "Location", "Transaction Type", "ID", "Customer Full Name", "Due Date", "Amount", "Open Balance" (the chevron button sits before "Date"): every word starts with a capital letter, as on the A/P reports.
  Step 2:
    - The PDF downloads.
  Step 3:
    - The PDF's column headings read exactly as on screen, capitals included.
  Step 4:
    - The CSV downloads.
  Step 5:
    - The heading row (right after the "As of: " and "Locations: All locations" rows) holds the same headings, letter for letter as on screen, and an "Aging Bucket" column for the bucket name ("Customer Full Name" is already among the headings).

### S3-03 | A/R Summary: the PDF and CSV match the rows and order on screen
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have open documents in these reports at two locations, location A and location B, with location A's name first in A to Z order.
  P: Make sure you are enrolled at both locations.
  P: Have two customers, customer A and customer B, with open documents at both locations on today's date, at least two each, with different amounts.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Summary (Reports, then "A/R Aging Summary" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: Leave the report with the Customer filter showing customer A and customer B ticked.
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Click the "Total" header once.
  2. Choose "Download (PDF)" from the ⋯ menu.
  3. Open the downloaded PDF and compare its rows with the screen, top to bottom.
  4. Choose "Download (CSV)" from the ⋯ menu.
  5. Open the CSV in a spreadsheet program and compare its rows with the screen.
  6. Without refreshing the page, click the customer name column (the first column) header.
  7. Choose "Download (CSV)" from the ⋯ menu again.
  8. Open the new CSV and compare its rows with the screen.
  Step 1:
    - The rows sort by Total, largest first.
  Step 2:
    - The PDF downloads as a file.
    - The "Success" message "Data exported successfully." shows.
  Step 3:
    - The PDF holds exactly the rows on screen for customer A and customer B, in the same order, and has a "Location" column, as the screen does (two locations are in view).
  Step 4:
    - The CSV downloads.
    - The "Success" message "Data exported successfully." shows.
  Step 5:
    - The CSV holds exactly the same rows in the same order, with a "Location" column.
  Step 6:
    - The rows re-sort by name.
  Step 7:
    - The CSV downloads with the "Success" message.
  Step 8:
    - The new CSV follows the new on-screen order, by name, not the earlier Total order.

    - The PDF downloads as a file.
    - The "Success" message "Data exported successfully." shows.
  Step 3:
    - The PDF holds exactly the rows on screen for customer A and customer B, in the same order, and has a "Location" column, as the screen does (two locations are in view).
  Step 4:
    - The CSV downloads.
    - The "Success" message "Data exported successfully." shows.
  Step 5:
    - The CSV holds exactly the same rows in the same order, with a "Location" column.
  Step 6:
    - The rows re-sort by Date.
  Step 7:
    - The CSV downloads with the "Success" message.
  Step 8:
    - The new CSV follows the new on-screen order, by Date, not the earlier Open Balance order.
### S3-06 | A/P Summary: the PDF and CSV match the rows and order on screen
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.

  5. Open the CSV in a spreadsheet program and compare its rows with the screen.
  6. Without refreshing the page, click the vendor name column (the first column) header.
  7. Choose "Download (CSV)" from the ⋯ menu again.
  8. Open the new CSV and compare its rows with the screen.

    - The rows sort by Total, largest first.

    - The PDF downloads as a file.
    - The "Success" message "Data exported successfully." shows.

### S3-09 | A/R Summary: each export states the as-of date and the filters
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have open documents in these reports at two locations, location A and location B, with location A's name first in A to Z order.
  P: Make sure you are enrolled at both locations.
  P: Make sure customer A and customer B each have invoices at location A that were 31 to 60 and more than 90 days past due on Oct 15, 2026.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Summary (Reports, then "A/R Aging Summary" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: Leave the report with the as-of date set to 10/15/2026 and only location A ticked in the Location filter (the Location column is hidden).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. In the Past due filter, tick "91+ days", then "31-60 days".
  2. In the Customer filter, tick customer B, then customer A.
  3. Choose "Download (PDF)" from the ⋯ menu.
  4. Open the PDF and read the lines under its title.
  5. Choose "Download (CSV)" from the ⋯ menu.
  6. Open the CSV and read the lines at the top.
  Step 1:
    - Both ranges are ticked.
  Step 2:
    - Both customers are ticked.
    - The chip reads "Customer: 2 customers".
  Step 3:
    - The PDF downloads.
  Step 4:
    - The lines read "As of Oct 15, 2026", then "Locations: " and location A's name, then "Past due: 31-60 days, 91+ days" (menu order, not the order ticked), then "Customers: " and customer B, customer A (the order ticked).
    - The PDF has no Location column.
  Step 5:
    - The CSV downloads.
  Step 6:
    - The CSV starts with four rows of one line each, in this order: "As of: Oct 15, 2026", then "Locations: " and location A's name, then "Past due: 31-60 days, 91+ days", then "Customers: " and customer B, customer A.
    - The column heading row follows directly.
    - There is no Location column.

    - The CSV downloads.
  Step 6:
    - Its Locations row reads the same: "Locations: ", location A's name, a comma and a space, then location C's name.
### S3-18 | A/R Summary: the CSV is one flat table with plain two-decimal amounts
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have a customer, customer A, with open invoices on today's date of $1,250.50 that is 1 to 30 days past due and $99.00 that is more than 90 days past due, and nothing in the other buckets.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Summary (Reports, then "A/R Aging Summary" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Choose "Download (CSV)" from the ⋯ menu.
  2. Open the CSV in a plain text editor (not a spreadsheet program).
  3. Choose "Download (PDF)" from the ⋯ menu.
  4. Open the PDF and look at its last bucket heading.
  Step 1:
    - The CSV downloads.
  Step 2:
    - The file starts with the "As of: " row and the "Locations: All locations" row, then the heading row, which reads the on-screen headings letter for letter, its last bucket column headed "91 And Over", exactly as the screen shows it.
    - One row for customer A: 1-30 Days reads 1250.50 and 91 And Over reads 99.00 (no $ sign, no thousands comma, two decimals).
    - Current, 31-60 Days and 61-90 Days are blank cells, not 0.
    - The Total reads 1349.50 (1250.50 + 99.00).
    - One Totals row at the bottom, only one.
    - There is no group header or subtotal row: every row except the Totals row is one customer.
  Step 3:
    - The PDF downloads.
  Step 4:
    - The PDF heading reads "91 And Over" on one line.

    - There is no group header or subtotal row: every row except the Totals row is one vendor.
  Step 3:
    - The PDF downloads.
  Step 4:
    - The PDF heading reads "91 And Over" on one line.

  Step 1:
    - The computer is offline.
    - The report stays on screen.
  Step 2:
    - The menu opens.
  Step 3:
    - The error message "We couldn't export this report. Please try again." shows: exactly one message, no second generic error.
    - Nothing is downloaded (no new file in the browser's downloads).
  Step 4:
    - The connection is back.
    - A new export works again.

### S4-01 | A/R Detail: the top-left chevron collapses and expands every group

  P: Make sure the report has open invoices, credit memos and deposits in at least three aging buckets on today's date.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Detail (Reports, then "A/R Aging Detail" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).

  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".

  2. Hover over the chevron button in the table's top-left header cell.
  3. Click that chevron button.
  4. Hover over the chevron button again.
  5. Click it.
  Step 1:
    - That bucket expands.
    - Its own chevron turns from pointing right to pointing down.
  Step 2:
    - The button's chevron points down (a group is expanded).
    - A tooltip above the button reads "Collapse all".
  Step 3:
    - Every group collapses.
    - The column under the button was not sorted: the sort mark is still on the column the report opened on.
  Step 4:
    - The chevron now points right.
    - The tooltip reads "Expand all".
  Step 5:
    - Every group expands.
    - The column under the button is still not sorted.
    - Every bucket row's own chevron points down (open).

### S4-09 | A/R Detail: the group row stays pinned under the headings
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Make sure that on today's date the report has two buckets, bucket A and the next bucket below it, bucket B, each with more lines than fit on the screen.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Detail (Reports, then "A/R Aging Detail" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: Leave the report with the groups of bucket A and bucket B expanded.
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Scroll down slowly into bucket A's rows.
  2. Keep scrolling until bucket B's row reaches the top.
  3. Keep scrolling into bucket B's rows.
  4. Scroll back up slowly until you are inside bucket A's rows again.
  5. Scroll down fast through bucket A's and bucket B's rows.
  6. Scroll fast back up through bucket B's and bucket A's rows.
  Step 1:
    - Bucket A's row stays pinned just under the column headings while its rows scroll beneath it.
    - It shows the bucket's chevron (pointing down) and its label: the bucket's name and count.
    - It shows no amounts (the bucket's sums stay in its own subtotal row).
  Step 2:
    - Bucket B's row pushes bucket A's row up and off and takes its place.
    - Only one group row is pinned at a time.
  Step 3:
    - Bucket B's row stays pinned.
  Step 4:
    - The change reverses: bucket A's row comes back down into the pinned place.
  Step 5:
    - At speed too, each pinned row is pushed up and replaced cleanly: it never covers the column headings or the Totals row at the bottom.
    - Two group rows never overlap.
  Step 6:
    - The same going back up: each pinned row is replaced cleanly, never covers the column headings or the Totals row.
    - Two group rows never overlap.

### S4-10 | A/R Collection: the group row stays pinned under the headings
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Make sure your role has the "View and Manage AP/AR Data" switch on.
  P: Make sure that on today's date the report has two customers next to each other in name order, customer A then customer B, each with more overdue invoices than fit on the screen and with a phone number and a credit term on its customer card.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Collection (Reports, then "A/R Aging Collection" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: Leave the report with the groups of customer A and customer B expanded.
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Scroll down slowly into customer A's rows.
  2. Keep scrolling until customer B's row reaches the top.
  3. Keep scrolling into customer B's rows.
  4. Scroll back up slowly until you are inside customer A's rows again.
  5. Scroll down fast through customer A's and customer B's rows.
  6. Scroll fast back up through customer B's and customer A's rows.
  Step 1:
    - Customer A's row stays pinned just under the column headings while its rows scroll beneath it.
    - It shows the customer group's chevron (pointing down) and its label: customer A's name and count, then "Phone: " and the phone number and "Credit term: " and the credit term.
    - It shows no amounts (the customer group's sums stay in its own subtotal row).
  Step 2:
    - Customer B's row pushes customer A's row up and off and takes its place.
    - Only one group row is pinned at a time.
  Step 3:
    - Customer B's row stays pinned.
  Step 4:
    - The change reverses: customer A's row comes back down into the pinned place.
  Step 5:
    - At speed too, each pinned row is pushed up and replaced cleanly: it never covers the column headings or the Totals row at the bottom.
    - Two group rows never overlap.
  Step 6:
    - The same going back up: each pinned row is replaced cleanly, never covers the column headings or the Totals row.
    - Two group rows never overlap.

### S4-15 | A/R Detail does not remember expanded groups

  P: Open A/R Detail (Reports, then "A/R Aging Detail" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Note which groups are expanded.
  2. Click the chevron on the first bucket row to change it.
  3. Open Sales By Customer (Reports, then "Sales By Customer" under PERFORMANCE in the left menu).
  4. Open A/R Detail (Reports, then "A/R Aging Detail" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu) again.
  Step 1:
    - Note the report's opening state.
  Step 2:
    - That bucket changes (expands or collapses).
  Step 3:
    - Sales By Customer opens.
  Step 4:
    - A/R Detail opens in the same state as in step 1, not the state you left: the change is not remembered.

### S4-17 | A/R Detail: changing the date or a filter collapses every group
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have open documents in these reports at two locations, location A and location B, with location A's name first in A to Z order.
  P: Make sure you are enrolled at both locations.
  P: Have a customer, customer A, with open invoices at both locations, some 31 to 60 days past due, on today's and yesterday's date.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Detail (Reports, then "A/R Aging Detail" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: Leave the report with every group expanded (Expand all).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Change the as-of date to yesterday.
  2. Click the top-left chevron button to expand every group.
  3. In the Customer filter, tick customer A.
  4. Click the top-left chevron button to expand every group.
  5. In the Past due filter, tick "31-60 days".
  6. Click the top-left chevron button to expand every group.
  7. In the Location filter, untick location B.
  Step 1:
    - The report reloads for yesterday with every group collapsed.
  Step 2:
    - Every group expands.
  Step 3:
    - Only customer A's rows remain.
    - Every group is collapsed.
  Step 4:
    - Every group expands.
  Step 5:
    - Every group collapses.
  Step 6:
    - Every group expands.
  Step 7:
    - Every group collapses.

### S5-01 | A/R Summary: the filter row reads As of, Customer, Past due, Location
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have open documents in these reports at two locations, location A and location B, with location A's name first in A to Z order.
  P: Make sure you are enrolled at both locations.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Summary (Reports, then "A/R Aging Summary" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Read the filter row under the title, chip by chip, left to right, letter for letter, capitals included.
  Step 1:
    - Four chips, in this order: the date chip "As of: " and today's date (for example "As of: 10/15/2026"), the Customer chip, the Past due chip, the Location chip.
    - The date chip says "As of", no longer "Date".
    - The Customer chip reads "Customer: All customers" with a person icon.
    - The Past due chip reads "Past due: Any" with an hourglass icon.
    - Both are in the resting (grey) style.
    - The Location chip reads "Location: All locations" with the location pin icon.
    - Every label matches exactly, capitals and punctuation included.

    - Four chips, in this order: the date chip "As of: " and today's date (for example "As of: 10/15/2026"), the Customer chip, the Past due chip, the Location chip.
    - The date chip says "As of", no longer "Date", and on this report the Customer chip now comes after it.
    - The Customer chip reads "Customer: All customers" with a person icon.
    - The Past due chip reads "Past due: Any" with an hourglass icon.
    - Both are in the resting (grey) style.
    - The Location chip reads "Location: All locations" with the location pin icon.
    - Every label matches exactly, capitals and punctuation included.

### S5-07 | A/R Detail: the Location menu lists the locations, ticked ones first
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have open documents in these reports at two locations, location A and location B, with location A's name first in A to Z order.
  P: Make sure you are enrolled at both locations.
  P: Make sure the report's Location list has 8 locations or fewer on today's date.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Detail (Reports, then "A/R Aging Detail" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Click the Location chip and read the menu from top to bottom.
  2. Untick location A.
  3. Close the menu.
  4. Click the Location chip again.
  5. Click "Clear selection".
  6. Click "Select all".
  Step 1:
    - A "Select all" row, a count of the ticked locations ("2 selected"), location A and location B, both ticked, and "Clear selection".
  Step 2:
    - The count reads "1 selected".
  Step 3:
    - The menu closes.
  Step 4:
    - The menu opens again: the ticked location, location B, is listed first, then location A.
    - There is no search box (8 locations or fewer).
  Step 5:
    - No location is ticked.
    - The chip reads "Location: No locations".
  Step 6:
    - Every location is ticked again.
    - The chip reads "Location: All locations".

### S5-10b | A/R Collection: the filters do not narrow the Location list
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have open documents in these reports at two locations, location A and location B, with location A's name first in A to Z order.
  P: Make sure you are enrolled at both locations.
  P: Have a customer, customer A, with open documents only at location A.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Collection (Reports, then "A/R Aging Collection" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).

  1. In the Customer filter, tick customer A.
  2. Click the Location chip and read the list.
  3. In the Past due filter, tick "1-30 days".
  4. Click the Location chip and read the list.
  5. In the Location menu, untick location B.
  6. Close the menu.
  7. Click the Location chip again and read the list.
  Step 1:
    - Only customer A's rows remain.
  Step 2:
    - Both locations are still listed: the list is not narrowed by the Customer filter.
  Step 3:
    - The chip reads "Past due: 1-30 days".
  Step 4:
    - Both locations are still listed: the list is not narrowed by the Past due filter.
  Step 5:
    - Location B is unticked.
  Step 6:
    - The menu closes.
  Step 7:
    - Location B is still in the list, unticked: the list is not narrowed by the Location filter.

### S5-15 | A/R Detail: each row belongs to its own document's location
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have open documents in these reports at two locations, location A and location B, with location A's name first in A to Z order.
  P: Make sure you are enrolled at both locations.
  P: Make sure customer A has, on today's date, an invoice A made at location A for $500.00 and partly paid ($200.00) by a payment taken at location B, a credit memo A made at location B and a deposit A taken at location B.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Detail (Reports, then "A/R Aging Detail" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: Leave the report with the Customer filter showing customer A ticked and every bucket expanded.
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Read the Location column of invoice A, credit memo A and deposit A.
  2. In the Location menu, untick location B.
  3. Expand every bucket and read the rows.
  4. Open the Location menu.
  5. Tick location B.
  6. Untick location A.
  7. Expand every bucket and read the rows.
  Step 1:
    - Invoice A shows location A.
    - Credit memo A and deposit A show location B.
  Step 2:
    - The report narrows to location A.
    - The Location column is hidden.
  Step 3:
    - Only invoice A remains, with its open balance of $300.00 ($500.00 − $200.00): the payment taken elsewhere does not move it.
  Step 4:
    - The Location menu opens.
  Step 5:
    - Location B is ticked again.
  Step 6:
    - Location A is unticked.
    - The report narrows to location B.
  Step 7:
    - Only credit memo A and deposit A remain.

### S5-19 | A/R Collection: ticked locations survive reload, Back and a shared link
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have open documents in these reports at two locations, location A and location B, with location A's name first in A to Z order.
  P: Make sure you are enrolled at both locations.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Collection (Reports, then "A/R Aging Collection" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. In the Location menu, untick location B.
  2. Copy the page address.
  3. Reload the page.
  4. In the Location menu, click "Select all".
  5. Click the browser's Back button.
  6. Open a new browser tab.
  7. Paste the copied address into its address bar.
  8. Press Enter.
  Step 1:
    - The report narrows to location A.
  Step 2:
    - The address is copied.
  Step 3:
    - The report reloads with only location A ticked.
  Step 4:
    - Every location is ticked.
  Step 5:
    - The report shows only location A ticked again.
  Step 6:
    - A new, empty tab opens.
  Step 7:
    - The copied address shows in the address bar.
  Step 8:
    - The shared link opens the report with only location A ticked.
### S5-20 | A/P Summary: a link naming several locations or every location
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Make sure the organization has open A/P documents at three locations, location A, location B and location C, and you are enrolled at all three.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/P Summary (Reports, then "A/P Aging Summary" under ACCOUNTS PAYABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. In the Location menu, untick location C.
  2. In the address bar, replace the two "locationIds=" entries with one: "locationIds=" followed by the two codes separated by a comma.
  3. Press Enter.
  4. In the Location menu, click "Select all".
  5. Untick location A.
  6. Untick location B.
  7. In the address bar, replace location C's code with the three codes separated by commas: location A's and location B's (noted in step 1), then location C's.
  8. Press Enter.
  Step 1:
    - The report narrows to location A and location B.
    - The page address carries the two ticked locations as two "locationIds=" entries, each followed by a long code (the location's code, not its name).
    - Note the two codes.
  Step 2:
    - The address bar shows one "locationIds=" entry with the two codes separated by a comma.
  Step 3:
    - The link listing locations separated by commas opens the report with those two ticked ("Location: 2 locations").
  Step 4:
    - Every location is ticked and the address no longer lists locations.
  Step 5:
    - Location A is unticked.
  Step 6:
    - Only location C is ticked.
    - The address holds one "locationIds=" entry: location C's code.
  Step 7:
    - The address bar shows the three codes separated by commas.
  Step 8:
    - A link naming every location opens as "Location: All locations".

  Step 2:
    - The column is labelled exactly "Location" (no longer "Location Name") and sits right after "Date".
    - "No." and "Vendor Name" keep their names.
  Step 3:
    - The column is labelled exactly "Location" (no longer "Location Name") and sits right after "Date".
    - "No." and "Vendor Name" keep their names.
### S6-03 | A/R Collection: the Location column shows with two or more locations
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Make sure the organization has open A/R documents at three locations, location A, location B and location C.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Collection (Reports, then "A/R Aging Collection" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Look for the Location column.
  2. Untick location C in the Location menu.
  3. Untick location B as well.
  4. Click "Clear selection".
  Step 1:
    - The Location column is shown (All locations, three in the list).
  Step 2:
    - The Location column is shown (two ticked).
  Step 3:
    - The Location column is hidden (one ticked).
  Step 4:
    - The Location column is hidden (none ticked).
    - The empty state shows.

  P: Open A/P Detail (Reports, then "A/P Aging Detail" under ACCOUNTS PAYABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Hover over the info icon right of the title.
  2. Move the pointer away.
  3. Press Tab until the info icon has keyboard focus.
  4. Press Escape.
  Step 1:
    - A tooltip shows, word for word: "Unpaid bills and unused vendor credits as of the selected date, grouped by how overdue they are."
  Step 2:
    - The tooltip closes.
  Step 3:
    - The same tooltip shows.
  Step 4:
    - The tooltip closes.

### S8-08 | A/R Detail: Clear all filters keeps the date and resets the rest
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have open documents in these reports at two locations, location A and location B, with location A's name first in A to Z order.
  P: Make sure you are enrolled at both locations.
  P: Have a customer, customer A, with open invoices only at location B, none 61 to 90 days past due, on yesterday's date.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Detail (Reports, then "A/R Aging Detail" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: Leave the report with the as-of date set to yesterday, only customer A ticked in the Customer filter, "61-90 days" ticked in the Past due filter and only location A ticked in the Location filter.
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Read the table area.
  2. Click "Clear all filters".
  Step 1:
    - "No record matches these filters", "Try changing filters to widen your results." and a "Clear all filters" button.
  Step 2:
    - Rows show again.
    - The as-of date is still yesterday.
    - The chips read "Customer: All customers", "Past due: Any" and "Location: All locations" (every location ticked).

### S8-10 | A/R Collection: a report that fails to load says so in place of the rows
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Make sure you can switch this computer's network connection off and on (such as Wi-Fi).
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Collection (Reports, then "A/R Aging Collection" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Switch the computer's network connection off.
  2. Change the as-of date to yesterday.
  3. Switch the network back on.
  4. Change the as-of date back to today.
  Step 1:
    - The computer is offline.
  Step 2:
    - The report tries to load and fails: in place of the rows it shows "Something went wrong loading this report. Please try again.", with the standard error message as well.
  Step 3:
    - The computer is online again.
  Step 4:
    - The report loads normally again.

### S9-01 | A/R Collection: each customer row shows phone and credit term
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Make sure your role has the "View and Manage AP/AR Data" switch on.
  P: Have a customer, customer A, with an overdue invoice, a phone number and a credit term on its customer card.
  P: Have a customer, customer B, with an overdue invoice and no credit term.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Collection (Reports, then "A/R Aging Collection" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Read customer A's group row.
  2. Open customer A's customer card in another tab and compare the phone and credit term.
  3. Switch back to the report's tab and read customer B's group row, and look for an email address or a contact name on either row.
  Step 1:
    - After the name: "Phone: " and the phone number, then "Credit term: " and the credit term.
  Step 2:
    - Both read exactly as the customer card shows them.
  Step 3:
    - Customer B's row reads "Credit term: COD" (none is set).
    - Neither row shows an email address or a primary contact.

### S7-20 | A/R Detail: the × on the switch prompt leaves the report as it was
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Have open documents in these reports at two locations, location A and location B, with location A's name first in A to Z order.
  P: Make sure you are enrolled at both locations.
  P: Have a customer, customer A, with open invoices at location A and one open work order invoice, invoice A, made at location B.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Detail (Reports, then "A/R Aging Detail" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: Leave the report with customer A ticked, the "Amount" header clicked once, every bucket expanded and the page scrolled so invoice A is in the middle of the screen.
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Copy the page address.
  2. Note which rows are at the top and bottom of the screen.
  3. Click invoice A's number.
  4. Click the "×" at the dialog's top right.
  5. Compare the page address with the copied one.
  Step 1:
    - The address is copied.
  Step 2:
    - Noted.
  Step 3:
    - The "Switch location?" dialog opens, with a close "×" at its top right.
  Step 4:
    - The dialog closes.
    - No message pops up.
    - You stay on the report exactly where you were: customer A still ticked, the Amount sort, every bucket still expanded and the same rows at the top and bottom of the screen (the same scroll position).
    - The location switcher in the top right still shows location A.
  Step 5:
    - The address is the same.
### DATA-01 | A/R Summary: 30 customers at a time, none repeated or skipped
  P: Sign in as an Admin user (the "Admin" role), with location A as your current location.
  P: Make sure that between 31 and 90 customers have open documents on today's date.
  P: Use a desktop browser window 1440 px wide.
  P: Open A/R Summary (Reports, then "A/R Aging Summary" under ACCOUNTS RECEIVABLE in the left menu).
  P: If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
  1. Choose "Download (CSV)" from the ⋯ menu.
  2. Open the CSV and count the customers in it (each name once).
  3. Count the customers shown before any more load (or compare them with the first 30 names in the CSV) and note the last one.
  4. Scroll down until the next customers load.
  5. Keep scrolling to the end of the list, writing down each customer's name.
  6. Click the "Total" header.
  7. Scroll from the top to the end of the list again, writing down each name.
  8. In the Past due filter, tick "1-30 days".
  9. Tick "91+ days".
  10. Scroll from the top to the end of the list again, writing down each name.
  Step 1:
    - The CSV downloads.
  Step 2:
    - Note the number of customers (for example 46).
  Step 3:
    - Exactly 30 customers.
  Step 4:
    - The next customers load below as you scroll (there are no page buttons), starting with the customer that comes right after the last one noted in step 3, not a repeat.
  Step 5:
    - Every customer appears exactly once and the count equals the CSV's.
  Step 6:
    - The rows sort by Total, largest first.
  Step 7:
    - In this sort too, every customer appears exactly once and none is missing.
  Step 8:
    - The chip reads "Past due: 1-30 days" and the report reloads with that range only.
  Step 9:
    - The chip reads "Past due: 2 ranges" and the report reloads with both ranges.
  Step 10:
    - Every customer left by the filter appears exactly once.
    - None repeats as more load.
